[[Congratulation with the greens.]] This makes a huge difference, which leads exactly to the opposite of what was originally intended. This phenomenon is well known in the economy; I am not saying anything new here. Every one of you who has enjoyed half a good national economic training is well-known, under different keywords. In American textbooks, it is usually called “paradox of thrift”, the paradox of sparingness, if you want to do so. If a household saves, it's good, but if everyone increases their savings ratio, what will that lead to? – This will mean that under the same circumstances the social product will fall, the national income will fall and can be saved even less from the reduced national income than before. ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e11056	Mr President. High house! [[Call to the ÖVP:Do you have federal party today?]] Today, from my point of view, certain language confusion has always arisen. Today, some talk about saving. Mr Katzian of the SPÖ says that the problems in Europe have arisen because the countries are now being broken down. Everyone wants to save, everyone saves, and that's why we have the problems we have. Is it really the case that the countries are saving? Who saves in Europe? Do you know anyone? Do you know anyone who saves in Europe? So I only know countries in Europe that are making more and more debts. It may be that some countries are trying to make a little less of it. That may be, and that is also very useful from my point of view, because if we continue to do so and make more and more debts, then exactly what everyone can calculate with a little bit of home mind happens. For example, if a private person improves their salary accordingly and makes more debts each month in order to increase the standard of living, then at some point there will be such a large mountain of debt that it no longer goes on. That's where we're right now. It's not like we have surpluses we want to put aside. No, it's about loaning ourselves money, permanent lending, and those who lend us the money don't want to lend it to us anymore, because they're afraid to get it back. That's exactly the point. That is, the decision we can make now is: Either we simply continue as before – as the SPÖ wants – or we make fewer debts, less new debts. There is no mention of debt reduction anyway. It is said again and again – even from the government I have already heard that – that we are reducing debt, but that is not happening. For the first time debt reduction is planned for 2018 or 2020. After Felder, debt is in fact reduced only in 2022. At the moment, it's just about whether we want to make a little less debt. Of course, it is easy to continue as before, of course it would be nice, no question. We're just making debts. We'll borrow the money from those who have it. That also leads me to another question. Someone once asked me: If all the world has debts, who does all the world owe? How's that? If everyone's in debt, who does everyone owe? The answer is simple: All the world is in debt that has capital. These are simple savers whose savings were made over the banks in government bonds – but the least savers know – these are pension funds, each of which hopes to receive a pension sometime, and so on. That means there are people behind it. They are people who have given these states money, and these people want their money back. This is not an evil financial speculator that can be sent into the desert, but there are people standing there, there are fates behind it. And if we continue as the Greens and the SPÖ call it, just make debt on devils come out, then all these people will lose their money. You have to know. These are people who are dependent on it in the pension, these are people who have been a little bit aside for bad times. They all lose their money if the states don't repay the capital – and we're heading right there. That is why there is in fact no alternative to – I do not say now, to the non-space, because we do not save – less-debt-making. There is no alternative to the less-debt-making process, otherwise the debt mountain grows even more over our heads, and then we are where Greece is at the moment. I'll tell you one thing: I'm sorry about the Greeks. It may be that the Greeks are going through a hard time now. That may be true, but in the past the Greeks have also benefited greatly from the money they have borrowed, from the money they did not have and borrowed in order to increase their economic performance accordingly in order to achieve a certain prosperity. The Greeks now get the invoice presented for this – and we will also get it presented. Let us look at what we have listed in recent years! In the last two decades, on average, we have recorded more economic performance for each euro 4 € more debt. Can anyone imagine that? Is that a viable concept? Can we continue like this? More economic performance 4 € more debt for each euro! Where that leads, everyone can calculate. That's why: Please wake up, you from the SPÖ and above all you from the Greens! It is not about saving, but it is about finally making fewer debts so that we can come back on a stable path and find people who lend us money again. Because that's what it's all about: We need people who save and then make the money available to us, and we only find these if we consolidate accordingly. [[President Neugebauer gives the bell sign.]] That's why: Don't save, but make fewer debts! There is no alternative. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e11425	We're coming to the 1st. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Wittmann is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e11452	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! This item on the agenda is really a special one, which is why I can remind you very briefly of the law making, of history. The debate started 25 years ago, I myself took part in this discussion for 15 years, and this discussion continued every year. It then came to one – let us say so – very important opinion in the Convention. There, the basic agreement of all parties on this issue has been established for the first time. Finally, this has led to intensive negotiations and has now been manifesting the largest administrative reform since 1920 in a request which, as it turns out, receives a five-party agreement. I think that's a difficult thing for you. The subject, for such a huge revolution in the area of federal constitution, is a truly unique step. I would therefore like to express my thanks to all the constitutional speakers of all parties. An extremely constructive procedure was chosen here, an extremely competitive procedure in the service of the matter. I would particularly like to thank my counterpart, the coalition partner Wolfgang Gerstl, who also acted very prudently. I also do not want to hide the fact that Mr Ostermayer's task is very important: to get the countries into the boat. You know, it wasn't quite easy with that matter. That is why I would like to thank you for doing a great job bringing the countries into the boat. Finally, but last but not least, I would like to thank a really excellent team of officials who have done the really difficult formulation work, and especially the Constitutional Service of the Federal Chancellor's Office. [[General condolences.]] We have also taken an unusual course in this matter, which documents that opposition and coalition parties have found themselves in a new way of cooperation. For the first time, a member of the opposition, namely Member of the Fichtenbauer House, will put forward an amendment to a government template which will receive the majority, and MP Musiol will put forward a motion for a resolution. They have made themselves particularly strong in these two areas, and it is very reasonable to regulate these topics separately. I believe that this procedure shows that we in the Constitutional Committee have an incredible potential for cooperation and a great willingness to do so. What's this about? – It is about transferring the administrative bodies, essentially the appeal body in second instance, to a court. One could have left the administrative bodies, but one has decided to use only one administrative authority and to settle the second instance with a court, such as the national administrative courts, or even the first instance already at a Federal Administrative Court and then to settle the other instance at the administrative court. That is to say, nine administrative courts have been set up, the national administrative courts in which the national administrations and the UVS commissions take place, and, so to speak, only a two-stage instance is created, but which has far greater legal certainty because independent judges will decide in second instance. This is actually a European standard, which we are now finally implementing in Austria. At the federal level, there will be two Federal Administrative Courts. One will deal with financial issues, the other will cover all other areas, so to speak. I would just like to say that we are disbanding 120 special authorities and moving them to a Federal Administrative Court. I believe that the special feature of the discussion in the Constitutional Committee was that no one of the parties present in this package, namely at the constitutional level, has called into question everyone in the boat. Since all these authorities are, so to speak, in a Federal Administrative Court of Justice with this Constitutional Act, we will also succeed in the greatest administrative reform since 1920. I think that was a really great performance in the committee. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] However, it has not only been allowed to introduce the actual administrative jurisdiction, but also to define the path of transposition into the simple legal provisions by eight resolutions and by eight committee decisions quite precisely, which is why this constitutional novel will go even further. In particular, a legislative complaint has developed from the discussion – and the way to do so until 20. June is given variants by the Constitutional Service – which is sent in review to have a transparent procedure to finally vote in autumn. I believe that this should only be pars pro toto, that the process of transposition into the simple legal provisions has already been specified quite precisely. That is why eight motions for resolutions and eight committee statements and an amendment have been tabled. The amendment is therefore important because now the Administrative Court can also decide on a reformatory basis, which makes sense. If the matter is raised and decision-making is ready, why should one refer it back to the instance whose decision is lifted? In the future, as has been said, the Administrative Court may decide independently. I believe that this will help to shorten the whole procedure and make sense. In essence, it can be said: Legal certainty has been increased, international standards have been met, many framework areas have been completed with this innovation. I think it is closer to the citizen that there are only two instances and that legal certainty is guaranteed in the second instance by judicial decision. The fact that there is a uniform training of judges and a uniform organisation has been covered in the motions for resolutions, which is likely to be possible. The basic principles of procedural law have also been laid down and an information law of the National Council has been adopted in the motion for a resolution so that we can be informed of how the implementation takes place. The fact that this implementation will concern 800 laws shows the extent of this innovation and also the extent of the work to be done in simple legal areas. It will therefore only come into force in 2014 because, of course, extensive preparatory work will have to be done in the Asylum Court, which will, as it were, represent the nucleus of the new Federal Administrative Court. It'll be a huge task. Finally, I would like to thank all those who have taken part in dealing with this task. It is a real reform of our constitution, the largest since 1920.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e11541	Next speaker: Mr Mag. Gerstl. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e11562	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Today, we are deciding on the largest administrative structural reform that the B-VG has probably been through since 1925. Since 1925, when the pressure of the League of Nations reduced the double-trackness of the administration in the countries, with the introduction of the office of the national governments, this is the structural reform that has been required by many people, many citizens in recent years and decades. We thus create a two-stage administrative jurisdiction, with the abolition of the administrative body, the abolition of more than 100 special authorities and, instead, the establishment of nine national administrative courts and two federal administrative courts. This will give countries jurisdiction for the first time. This is a demand of the countries for many, many years, and this also increases the position of the countries in our state structure. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] With this administrative jurisdiction, we also align our legal protection system with European standards. We are therefore fit for all future, foreseeable European requirements for a legal protection system. With this reform, we are implementing the structural reform so often required. That is why today, on which hopefully all five parties decide on this reform, is a good day for Austria, because this decision proves that major reforms are possible across all administrative structures in the federal government and in the countries and over all political parties. This is a good day for Austrian parliamentarianism, because this constitutional novel has been prepared for example. This amendment was not only discussed several times in the Committee, but also held hearings with experts and stakeholders, discussed with constitutional judges, whose views and the opinions of the different political parties were included in the text, which led to a very clear legislative work being brought together. It is likely that this bill is one of the best prepared we have ever had in this House. It is a good day for parliamentarianism, because it has succeeded in putting the common whole before the divider. I would particularly like to thank the opposition parties who have resisted the temptation to put party-political avants in the foreground, and to decide on this great structural reform together with us. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the SPÖ.]] But it is also a good day for Austria's business location, because this innovation leads to faster and better planning for the companies in Austria and to greater legal certainty. Finally, and finally, it is a good day for citizens because they get more clarity about the competent authorities and better and faster legal protection. Every decision in Austria is now taken by an independent judge in the ordinary instance, and that is what is to be celebrated today. For this I would like to say a special thank you, not only to my co-constitutional speaker Peter Wittmann, but above all to two people: to State Secretary Ostermayer and to Deputy Chancellor Michael Spindelegger. ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e11677	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! High house! I bring the amendment tabled by Mr Wittmann, Mag. Gerstl, Dr. Fichtenbauer, Mag. Daniela Musiolund Herbert Scheibnerreport to the Committee 1771 of the Supplements. The amendment is distributed, and I can only refer to the main points in the short term. Article 1, amendment of the Federal Constitutional Act, in accordance with the Committee Report, Article 7, amendment of the Environmental Impact Assessment Act, Article 9, amendment of the Administrative Court Act, and Article 10, amendment of the Constitutional Court Act. I cannot accept the detailed amendments to the wording, which would lead to great boredom, but I would ask that this amendment be debated and, subsequently, that it be decided. On the part of the Freedom Party, I can say that we have been involved with great effort, with great responsibility and with great seriousness in this debate and in this reform effort. The matter of administrative law and administrative procedural standards, I say only in a small historical trip, is a deeply internal matter of the third camp, but was the creator of the administrative procedural laws 1925, which are still in force, the great lawyer Egbert Mannlich, who, in close cooperation with Kelsen, also worked on the preparation of the federal constitution in 1920 and, as the section council of a newly established authority after 1918, designed the administrative procedure laws. He also participated in the constitutional amendment in 1925, which Mr Gerstl has already mentioned, but also in the Constitutional novel in 1929. This means that the area of administrative procedure is one of the genetic content of the third camp. Despite this historical fact, it has been a natural commandment for us to approach hot needle in such profound and substantial changes that affect the state and its administration, as it has so beautifully called. With absolute joy – ‘joy’, I now ask you not to understand in the euphemistic sense – we have the right to take note, with the utmost seriousness and with the primacy of rationality, that the implementation of the project is a general concern of all five parliamentary parties. Therefore, it is probably right to point out that good things need time, but it was a high-quality energy that has been used by all parties, and it has been and is highly appreciated that under this, I say, management of Mr. State Secretary Dr. Ostermayer also the Constitutional Service of the Federal Chancellor's Office and the associated experts with a very high-quality intensity contributed positively to the work. With a special emphasis on the importance of this, I say that it is also very satisfactory that an important detail has also been implemented, namely that the request made by our group, Mr Stefan and I, in April 2010 that the Administrative Court will finally receive the reformatory competence in addition to the cassatorial competence. Of course, it is necessary to imagine the changes that have taken place in the shadows of public and media attention. This is not a sensational desire, envy and evilness, which are much more enjoyable to celebrate, revitalized, but a profoundly effective, very pragmatic revitalization. The Administrative Court is entitled to about 1,500 complaints per year. And if I estimate about 500 such cases are settled in the case itself – but there is no statistics on this, there can be so much or something less – that means that by exercising a reformatory possibility of the Administrative Court 500 to 700 administrative procedures are saved at a new level, because the cash will only be transferred downwards. These are millions and millions of millions who are totally in the shadow of the Austrian budget economy, but it leads to an absolutely clear administrative relief. It was also important to us in the motions for resolutions, which have been adopted almost unanimously, that the framework – Mr Wittmann and Mr Gerstl have already pointed out – should be completed, because the implementation of today's legislative decision would be too little. It is the essential additions to which the headlamp must also be cast: the creation of a uniform image at the quality level, which is also generated by the proper jurisdiction; Rules of procedure new, because the administrative rules of procedure are no longer sufficient for the counter-dictive new procedure; an administrative court organisation. And above all, there is another innovation – a year ago, MEPs Stefan and colleagues made a request on this matter – namely the introduction of the legislative complaint. The introduction of the case-law is intended to mean that a party concerned may, in ordinary court proceedings, request that the standard obtained by the Constitutional Court be reviewed for its constitutionality. If the application is manifestly unsubstantiated and hopeless, the Constitutional Court should, as now, be able to reject a senseless appeal in the simple tripartite path. If not, the Constitutional Court shall carry out the examination. This process is a very essential addition to the Austrian legal protection system. The ordinary courts already have the opportunity to accept a suggestion and to comply with it on submission to the Constitutional Court or not. The experience, which can be proved by individual examples, shows that it depends on absolutely non-predictable and rationally non-comprehensible circumstances in individual cases whether this suggestion is followed. The individual examples available show that, to this extent, the proper jurisdiction does not provide sufficient legal protection in order to express it cautiously. Therefore, this supplement, which needs to be taken as a result of this five-party resolution request, is an important measure. The jurisdiction on the other hand will be stormy, but that doesn't matter, we'll probably prove ourselves to be lawmakers. A big day for Austria. A great day for parliamentarianism, because there is unanimity in the big and important matter. It is certainly an opportunity to thank all the people who have played a decisive part in all the Members of the other political groups, all of them at the constitutional level, who have been able to do so. Heimo Probst, and also the other persons who care for the individual clubs in this direction, also the rulers of the Constitutional Service. And finally, Let us be glad that we live in this Republic! - Thank you.

This means that each time an authority leaves a decision, it will not be possible to call on any of these special authorities in the future, if one wants to oppose it, but that there will be nine national administrative courts and two federal administrative courts for the first time in Austria. Three criteria were very important to us both in the preparation of our own request and in the assessment of the ministerial draft. The one question was: Is legal protection guaranteed? Is this an improvement in legal protection? Is this an improvement for citizens in the sense of “cost-effective” or at least “not more expensive ”? For the purposes of: Can they defend against decisions taken by authorities or then by the first courts to act on them? Is this really an independent dish? – After all, it is an essential criterion for courts to ensure independence. And above all: Are there any significant allocations, whether there is something at a national administrative court or at a federal administrative court? We have already had a number of points which we have noted in the negotiations, which have not yet fit us and which were also crucial, depending on how they are going, whether we can agree or not. It was, in essence, a good draft, Mr State Secretary, but there were just a few points that were important to us. As you know, we have brought these into discussions with the other Members of the Constitutional Committee, but also into joint discussions with you. I am sure that is true of what Mr Wittmann, Mr Gerstl and all my previous speakers have said: The climate that prevailed in the negotiations – the mutual listening, the exchange of arguments and one another approach – was certainly an extraordinary one. One might even say: In fact, it should be a matter of course during negotiations in Parliament. But perhaps we have once laid a slat for other topics. But we have come closer to each other in many points, we can certainly say that. What was important to us? – For example, it was important that EIA procedures, i.e. environmental impact assessment procedures, do not remain in the individual countries, so that we would have had nine different case-laws and nine different handling procedures within Austria, but that there is at least a federal competence here, that is, a Federal Administrative Court is responsible. This was quite a discussion that had to be held with the national leaders, because it is now the case that such questions are also questions of power. But here there is now a clear agreement, here there is clearly the will that the Federal Administrative Court be responsible. This ensures that we receive, in one of the most essential procedures dealing with environmental compatibility, an Austrian-wide case law and not a fragmentation into the various federal states. Even better, of course, would be a European and global case law with regard to the environment, because we all know that does not end at the border, but of course we are far away from it. We are the Austrian Parliament here and can now only decide on that. Then there were many groups that have turned to us, namely to all parties. These were the lawyers, the notaries, the universities, various experts such as constitutional lawyers and administrative lawyers who have put certain points in the foreground here. I've already mentioned the universities: There, from the students to the professors, to the chairman of the Senate, everyone was concerned that this could lead to a deterioration in their participation in universities in the draft. After many conversations, creative moments of my colleague, Green-Constitutional Representative Marlies Meyer, we have come together to solutions that now, I believe, fit for everyone. It is thus ensured that universities have a role to play in their autonomy, but above all in their participation here. I have already mentioned independence. It was important to us that there is a real independence here, that is also clear: How does the transition happen now? How does the transition between those who work in the independent administrations of the countries go to judges? - There must be a procedure. Here, too, it must be clarified: There is a need for a transparent procedure, clear criteria need to be taken into account who becomes an administrator. who can and who can't. That is what we have just said in a joint motion. We have had to discuss longer when it comes to legal protection. One was the question of costs. There we were, I think, all relatively agreed that there should not be higher costs here, for example, that officials, i.e. persons working in authorities, should be used as a priority. Everyone who has ever had to deal with the court knows that experts are, so to speak, the major cost points in the proceedings. It was important to us that no higher costs were incurred both for procedures relating to students and for environmental impact assessment procedures or any other procedures covered by them. This means priority use of officials, which means that there is no legal force, and much more. But, of course, legal protection is not only a question of costs, but also of: How far can you go? What are the concerns of the Court of Justice? – It was provided for in the proposal that low benefits in money or money should not be allowed to go to the Administrative Court. You can't really imagine that now, even if you've studied it once, like me. What does that mean? – For example, social security legislation would have affected certain benefits. For example, if you were to get a wheelchair, you would no longer have been able to go to the administrative court because, after the presentation, it would have been a low performance in money or money value. That's what we've negotiated, and we've managed to make it change. The legislative complaint is also a long project, which has already been discussed at the Austrian Convention. There were two models back then. Contrary to all media reports, the following have been recorded: The law complaint was – in my opinion, I would have heard at least that from no party, so to speak, as a condition for it to agree – and the law complaint was equally important to all parties in the discussion. But we also said: It's not that fast to implement it right away. There you have to look at the expertise, there you have to look at different models. That is why there is a motion for a resolution here that the Federal Chancellery will present a proposal before the summer, which will then be tabled by all of us by means of an own-initiative proposal. At the latest by the end of the year, this legal complaint should have been implemented. What's the law complaint? – It is also the possibility that individuals may also oppose the use of a constitutional standard. That's not going to happen until now, but the court can do it. Later, this can also be done by the individual(s) affected. All in all, I think, good negotiations and a good package! It's not over yet. Now it is still about simple legal implementation. It will also be a matter of looking at the authorities, as they now exist, once again: What happens to the tasks that this Administrative Jurisdiction Novelle has not affected? They must continue to exist. What happens to these authorities? That is why we are putting forward the following motion for a resolution, which I am allowed to put forward here, a motion for a resolution by Mr Dr Peter Wittmann, Mr Mag. Wolfgang Gerstl, Dr. Peter Fichtenbauer, Mag. Daniela Musiol, Herbert Scheibner, colleagues. The Members who have been signed put to the vote the motion for a resolution: ‘The National Council wants to decide: The Federal Government is invited to implement the administrative jurisdiction on the occasion of the implementation of the in respect of those dissolved independent administrative authorities, to ensure that responsibilities which are not transferred to the administrative courts or assigned to the ordinary courts, and also cannot be delegated to the administrative courts; verify that those authorities are to be re-established or that these tasks are to be delegated to other authorities; ensure that the expertise available in these authorities is maintained through the involvement of experts and stakeholders; to submit appropriate legislative proposals in such a timely manner as to ensure the continuous provision of these tasks, in particular in cases where this is required by Union law, such as regulatory tasks or data protection matters.’ So, Mr State Secretary: Negotiating well with the countries! But there's still a lot to do, I'm saying, Health reform, school administration reform, federal competence in education. Maybe you want to get in there, too. If you can do so well with the countries, it might be helpful. [[Cheerfulness of Secretary of State Dr. Ostermayer.]] Or, for example, democracy reform would also be a point where we need this consensus. I am certainly looking forward to the unanimous decision, and I am also looking forward to your getting involved in further reforms. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e12535	Next speaker: Mrs Mag. Steßl-Mühlbacher. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e12557	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! With the resolution of more than 120 independent appeals with 1. We have achieved a great reform here in the National Council in January 2014 and the creation of national administrative courts in their place. For over two decades, an intensive effort has been made to introduce a two-stage administrative jurisdiction in Austria. Until now, this has failed mainly on competence and cost issues. I would like to join in thanking the previous speakers and the previous speaker. In particular, I would like to thank the constructive cooperation with the opposition parties, including the BZÖ, which will vote in spite of the amendment, for its constructive cooperation, of course also with the employees. In addition to the previously mentioned speakers in the SPÖ club, Mr Beppo Pointner, I should like to thank him for his work. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] What are our motives for implementing this reform now? It is primarily federalist and rule of law. In addition, the requirements of the European Convention on Human Rights and Union Law and in recent years the aim of the Administrative Court is also to relieve it. Our aim is to develop the legal protection system for citizens, to speed up procedures and to relieve the administrative court. With regard to the importance of the reform, I should like to ask Mr. Universitätsprofessor Dr. Holzinger, President of the Constitutional Court, from his section on the Austrian Law Day on 12 May. Cite May 2012. Dr. Holtinger said: "From the legal point of view, this will finally take account of the obligations arising from the European Convention on Human Rights, but also of the requirements of Union law. From a federal perspective, it should be emphasised that the countries will have a first share in the state function of jurisdiction. And in a state-organised way, the concentration of management on a single administrative body associated with the creation of two-stage administrative jurisdiction constitutes an important step in administrative reform.” What does this great litter look like, as we may call it, in detail? – In each federal state, an administrative court is established first instance, in the federal court a federal administration court and a federal finance court. They replace the independent administrative Senates of the countries, the independent financial Senate, the Federal Award Office, the Asylum Court as well as numerous special authorities of the federal government. In the countries too, numerous senior commissions are being replaced with this draft. On the occasion, the citizen, perhaps a little more understandable for the citizen, is a very, very dry matter: Someone who wants to call against a notice, for example against a building notice or a Tax ruling – except for municipal matters – no longer needs to be addressed to the next higher administrative authority, but can immediately refer to the independent administrative court. The highest instance remains in all administrative matters of the Administrative Court, which, in principle, is also responsible for asylum matters. This reform, ladies and gentlemen, brings new structures, speeding up procedures and, above all, slimming structures. The goal is with 1. January 2014 to start uniformly in federal and country countries. This is quite realistic on the basis of today's draft. It is also worth mentioning that, in this context, we are also submitting a motion for a resolution to the Chancellor of the Federal Republic of Germany to present us with a draft concerning the introduction of a legislative complaint. The competence of environmental impact assessment now becomes a federal issue, and there is also a reorganisation of the institution in the field of university regulations. All in all, ladies and gentlemen, this is a significant step forward, and I thank you on behalf of my group for the constructive cooperation. Thank you.

It would be absurd if a Lower Austrian national administration judge could not change to Vienna because there are other conditions there. We have to keep an eye on that. However, I recognise the will of the five parties to prevent that. Second point – and that is very important –: There is an agreement, there is a assurance that the highest sensitivity will be observed when ordering the tops of the courts. What cannot be done is – and this is unfortunately the case in Austria very often in God – that, when structures and government organisations are created, they look where they can move in person. There is a clear commitment that there is transparency in orders and that possible party-politically motivated orders are prevented. Third point – and I am glad to say that: It has been removed from the rule of law. I remember very well – I was a Member of Parliament, it was sometime four or five years ago – like Parliament in a case of a rule of law – how am I supposed to say? – Recognition of the instance in the asylum proceedings has cut off to the administrative court. This was unique because it created second-class legal protection seekers for the first time, namely all those who are in the asylum sector; in contrast to others, such as Häuselbauer et cetera, they are not allowed to go to the administrative court. That has been eliminated, and that is a good thing, a great success. Fourth point: Since many authorities are now acting in the field of Federal Administrative Courts, the opportunity arises in the area of data protection to create a new government structure that does not have the make-up of the old Data Protection Commission. On the one hand, this is about the necessary independence to the Federal Chancellery, organisational and personnel. You know that a infringement procedure is pending within the framework of the European Union. If you create a new data protection authority, you could see that you are implementing the EU in accordance with it. Secondly, the human resources of this new data protection authority are also appropriate to their increasingly important tasks. That's my great wish. The last point, which is also contained in the motion for a resolution, is very forward-looking and important to us: The extension of the position of legal parties in the administrative procedure. There are legal parties now. There are now, for example, environmental lawyers, environmental NGOs such as the WWF, who have a party position in the administrative procedure and have general interests in relation to environmental protection. The problem and the deficit is that these legal parties had no access to the Constitutional Court so far. This is a massive deficit because, of course, many of the things that have been claimed in these proceedings could have been claimed before the Constitutional Court. This is important because those who could go to the Constitutional Court often have a financial breath, but also as far as sustainability is concerned, so to speak, to end the dispute before the Constitutional Court. There are associations in a stronger position, and it is good that there are these associations. We should therefore take the final step, as well as in a motion for a resolution, that these legal parties can move before the Constitutional Court in accordance with a closed administrative procedure. I do not consider the question of the extension of the meaning of associational action to be limited to the administrative procedure. We also have this debate in consumer protection and labour law. We note that, for various reasons, individual parties who have rights sometimes find it difficult to sue these rights until the last instance, both economically and also because of a power gap. I think it would be an important contribution to start here with the legal parties in the administrative procedure, but to make the debate much wider. A proper legal protection must be a concern to Parliament, even if, of course, this sometimes slows down and makes it more difficult; but nothing is better than if the applicants for legal protection are sometimes given an appropriate framework to reach their rights. In this sense: one step after 20 years. May the next administrative reform not wait so long! - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e13192	The next speaker is Mrs Mag. Korun. 5 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e13213	Ladies and gentlemen! Mr. State Secretary! Dear guests at the gallery! There have already been many points which are significant improvements and which are to be addressed to Parliament today. I won't repeat it. I would like to refer to one of our main points in detail. There are also other points addressed by my previous speakers, by my colleagues Musiol and Steinhauser. It has been mentioned briefly that this amendment is intended to take a major, important step forward. It could well be described as a step “to the back”, because it takes back a strengthening which was introduced in 2008 at the same time as the creation of the asylum court, namely a two-class justice. In concrete terms, this concerns the problem of removing asylum seekers and asylum seekers from the right to lodge a complaint to the Administrative Court. This capping of access to the Administrative Court has not only created a two-class justice, i.e. persons, legal subjects, who in most cases are very likely to have the right to appeal to the Administrative Court if they believe that serious errors have occurred in their proceedings. Asylum seekers no longer had this possibility. This decision also had devastating consequences. It should be borne in mind that before access to the Administrative Court had at least one-fifth of the complaints to the Administrative Court, every fifth asylum seeker, who said that my application for asylum had been rejected for injustice, was entitled to the Administrative Court. The whole was introduced in addition to the cynical argument of process acceleration. A “accelerated” especially in cases of asylum, where it is often about life or death, whether the people concerned get protection from persecution or not, had very serious and very massive effects and still has to this day. It is therefore very gratifying that this large package, with this large throw, will also in principle reverse this 2008 step that access to the administrative court should also be possible in case of asylum complaints. This also gives us the possibility in the legal system that serious procedural errors or significant errors in the validation of evidence could be taken back into account by the Supreme Courts. I deliberately say “may” because it will also be very concrete to formulate the basic legal situation, whether this newly created access to the administrative court is actually one or not. And we will closely monitor whether what is now being decided by this amendment is also implemented in the corresponding simple laws, guaranteed and secured. At this point also a specific criticism: If you look at the law for the Federal Office for Asylum and Migration, which is currently under review, the assessment period will end tomorrow – then you can see that the draft assessment currently provides for a revision, i.e. a appeal to the highest courts only for the Ministry of the Interior, but not for the right-wing asylum seekers and asylum seekers. It will certainly not be able to stay. One cannot say on the one hand that we are creating access, we allow access to the administrative court in cases of asylum complaints again, and then through the back door, through the simple legal regulation, to reverse and cut back. What would not be allowed, which cannot be, is that, with placeholders' provisions, as they are currently in place in the relevant law, they should be smuggled into and written into the law, which will be discussed and debated here in the house in the coming weeks and months, and that they should be taken back to the administrative court. In short: We look forward to the many, many innovations and improvements in administrative jurisdiction. We have also negotiated a lot of points that will be taken into account today. As regards access to the Administrative Court, it must be guaranteed not only at constitutional level but also at a simple legal level. - Thank you.

The new law of service for the educational universities provides a significant improvement in the education of teachers and therefore in the long term also means a shift in quality at schools, because it is quite clear: The quality of teaching depends primarily on the persons acting, and that is what teachers are. If you really take the new teacher education seriously, you also need a custom service right. In this way, this law creates university conditions at the educational universities both from development and from quality. There has so far been no payment form for scientifically competent applicants who did not have a teaching office. With the newly created, multi-member use of teachers, people who are primarily interested in research, development or quality management can now also be employed. In future, it will also be possible to regulate the work of teachers not only in time units, but within a time frame model. It is necessary to have more capacity for research at the educational universities. This is also the need to comply with this law. This focuses on practical vocational research, which can be directly implemented in teaching. Thus, this law brings numerous positive changes, is a step in the right direction and is also a significant basis for further development of educational education. Especially with us in Vorarlberg, where, for example, the creation of a university of education will be made possible in the future, this right of service will put a barrier towards tertiary training or establishment. In my speech in connection with the Service Law Novelle, I would like to address an area that seems to me to be extremely important, namely the individual learning accompaniment at the New High Level and the New Middle School. In the context of the upper-level Neue, the salary law also provides for a pay scheme for those teachers who are used as part of the individual learning accompaniment. I consider the individual learning accompaniment to be one of the most important measures in the New High Level. It is essential in educational terms, in fact, the essential that the students are well encouraged, that their individual needs and abilities be addressed professionally, and that they are accompanied in their acquisition of knowledge. Therefore, the School Education Act provides for a so-called learning coach, a learning companion, to be established with a view to promoting and accompanying pupils who have learning residues and learning weaknesses. This should be individually focused on the strengths and weaknesses of the Students can enter and guarantee the best possible learning success. In the future, this will be achieved without supplementary education. The establishment of learning companions is also extremely important in social terms. This shows the current study of the Chamber of Workers, where it says: The remedial barometer is based on an IFES survey, where more than 2,700 families have been surveyed, and shows that over 127 million euros are spent for private re-help. It is deplorable that 270 000 children – 27 percent of them – need follow-up lessons. Of these, 70,000 children are not given remedial treatment because their parents cannot afford to do so. This means that four out of ten children do not receive appropriate follow-up instruction. 47 percent of families are very difficult and are under financial pressure when it comes to remedial expenditure. It is not surprising that this burden is highest among the socially weakest families. This social injustice must be prevented. Good means for this are the New Secondary School, the New High Level and the Learning Coach. One of the key points of the New High Level is the increase in the willingness of students to perform through individualisation of teaching, through individual support linked to a new learning and teaching culture. This is to be achieved through various measures. One is the voluntary learning accompaniment. This will be introduced gradually from 2013/2014 and will apply to all from 2017 onwards. Finally, I would like to stress that there is still a great desire for reform in school policy that we must be willing to reform. In any case, it is a step forward that the educational universities are moving closer to the universities, especially as regards teaching and research, but also the habilitation process. Thank you.

[[18:57:33]] 4. Point First reading: Request by Mrs Herbert Scheibner, Members of Parliament concerning a Federal Constitutional Act amending the Federal Constitutional Act in order to shift the competence for youth protection (1897/A)
ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e20044	We're coming to 4. Agenda item. The applicant spoke, Mr Scheibner. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e20068	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! They will remember that it has not been so long since, at least in the public, it was discussed very intensively that there should be uniform provisions on youth protection throughout Austria. The coalition parties also held this in their government programme. I would remind you that Mr Mitterlehner, Minister of Economic Affairs, has still said that, as an example, the thread of patience will soon come to him if there is no agreement with the countries in the near future. The fact is that a few weeks ago such an agreement was not reached, that the talks were held, that there would be no 15a agreement and that it was still different in Austria – depending on whether a 16-year-old lived in Burgenland, whether he lived in Carinthia, whether he lived in Vorarlberg, whether he lived in Vienna – as if he was considered to be mature, when he and where he was allowed to go, when he had to go home or how he was controlled. – All of this depends on where he has his residence or at least his stay. Ladies and gentlemen, at a time when we are talking a lot about the division of competence between the federal government and countries, where the federal states always think it is so important and meaningful that there is this national legislation, because that is part of the identity of the federal states and without it the centralists in Vienna are upgraded, and that cannot be allowed. - Time and again we hear that. And now, ladies and gentlemen, I ask you: Is it part of the identity of the Carinthians that they decide for themselves what a 16-year-old can and can't do, and that must be different from what the youth in Vorarlberg can do? Is this really necessary to define an identity? . I ask that because it seems to have failed in two federal states. I think no. There are no longer any arguments as to why this should be regulated differently. I say it quite honestly to you – and there are many other points, too, but we have made it clear on this point because it clearly shows the futility of such debates in itself. This is not just a matter of fact, but a lot of costs are related to all these provisions. There is a bureaucracy, there are people in the state governments, in the country days that deal with it, there are people who are responsible for control. All of this has to be financed jointly by all of us – only because in some federal states people are people who seem to justify themselves with it. In the Constitutional Convention, we discussed for a very long time what this legislative competence should be like. At that time, they said – not me, but many others, including professors: Yes, there should continue to be a national legislation, but it should be as uniform as possible. I think, especially in the field of youth protection, but also in many other areas, it is certainly understandable that this should be uniform. I'm really wondering: If the nine federal states – and there are so many reasonable people on the go – can not even agree on the field of youth protection – and this does not concern the fundamental festivals of the Republic and not the fundamental festivals of the federal states, how should they agree in other areas? If that is not the case – and I am now saying this with the self-confidence of the federal legislator and the Federal Constitutional Legislator – then they must be forced to have a reasonable, that is, uniform, regulation. [[BZÖ's condolences.]] Against this background – obviously it has been abandoned in the Federal Government that there are further negotiations on it – we say: The countries did not agree, good – or bad. We cannot accept this because we have an interest in a reasonable uniform system. We can all sit back and discuss what this should look like. They are all representatives of the federal states. I also oppose the fact that they say: The ones in Vienna – that is, all of us; I am Vienna, but most of you are not. That you can always say that you are the Wasserkopf Vienna, who does not know what the federal states want and what you can do for them here in Vienna, I do not understand. I would not like this as a member of the National Council. Against this background, this request should be understood. If the countries cannot agree on a reasonable arrangement, then we draw on this competence, negotiate it together and make a reasonable federal uniform youth protection rule. In this sense, I hope that we will discuss, negotiate and decide, as far as possible, unanimously and unanimously, in the Committee.

Perhaps in detail on the Federal State of Carinthia, because the impression is given that Carinthia – for Vorarlberg I can't judge it so precisely – is crossed here so that the Youth Protection Act is not bound up. That's what I'm rejecting. I've been calling the State Councillor Christian Ragger today. He says that the country of Carinthia is disturbing the fact that in the so-called East Solution, that is, in the federal states of Vienna, Lower Austria and Burgenland, heavy alcohols, i.e. spirits, were already completed 16 years ago. ................................................................................................................................................................ This is not in question for the Federal Republic of Carinthia. We can also win some of this. Let's just remember certain comas. There you have to be extremely careful. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] In general, we believe – and I am right to Mr Einwallner – that ultimately the protection of young people must be in the foreground and that we should not have great debates of competence here. Our youth needs special protection on one side of our living model. It is therefore a question of supporting and protecting young people through these youth protection laws, but of promoting the self-responsibility and independence of young people in the sense of leisure and living, and of limiting the freedom of our young people as little as possible. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] In this field of tension, modern youth protection policies are moving with clear and necessary rules to be defined here. The current situation looks like – we know – that there are nine national laws. I mentioned the so-called East Solution earlier. Vienna, Lower Austria and Burgenland eventually have the most liberal access to youth protection. As far as the field of residence or residence is concerned, I must admit that the nine laws of the country are an inconsistency. I would just like to give you one example: A 17-year-old Steirer crosses the border to celebrate legally in Lower Austria until the early hours of the morning, and then, when he comes home unaccompanied after two o'clock, becomes punishable in his own federal state, in Styria. Of course, this is not necessary. Or: Children up to 14 may travel in Vorarlberg and Salzburg until 23 o'clock, which is also clearly regulated. In Salzburg, however, this only applies if it is the night before Sundays and holidays. There are already a few – and you recognize that too – confusing and bizarre differences in the individual national laws, which you can certainly homogenize. As far as alcohol consumption is concerned, we note that there is more unity, except for the eastern solution, the three states quoted compared to the other federal states. In any case, it is regulated that until the end of the 16th. Year of life of the acquisition, possession and consumption of alcohol are prohibited. It's very important. That's all right. In the case of the high-percent spirits, the three quoted federal states are screeching out, and Carinthia rightly says: We're not playing with that. So we believe that all efforts to achieve harmonisation are useful, but ultimately it must be in the foreground that the nine federal states agree. Only when the agreement is there can we consider – or only then, of course, does it make sense – to give the protection of youth under federal sovereignty. But at the moment the agreement of the nine federal states is in the foreground, and that is a very crucial factor. [[Abg. Scheibner: And if they don't agree?]] – If You don't agree, Mr Scheibner, then we know we can't do anything either, it's useless. One cannot simply get over it without an agreement from the federal states. There must – and this is an accusation to the Federal Minister, who is not here today due to the first reading – spit the Federal Minister's hands and negotiate accordingly with the nine federal states. The will is there, the will is mainly in the state of Carinthia. I can assure you. In this sense a nice evening.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e20427	Next speaker: Mrs Windbüchler-Sushill. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-05-15-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00155_d7e20448	Mr President. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Back again: What is it about? First of all, it is about young people being able to act on their own within the framework of a Youth Protection Act. Secondly, We here represent the federal legislation, and we have every opportunity to pass federal laws. Again, the example: If the 15-year-olds – we call them Franziska – want to enjoy themselves a little bit at Semmering after having been snowboarding with their friends in après-ski life, they have to watch exactly which side it stands on – on one side the Styria, on the other side Lower Austria. So far, it is still possible to leave in Lower Austria until a clock and in the Styria only until 23 o'clock. This is just an example from all over Austria, which shows exactly what the protection of young people is. In fact, the protection of young people should provide legal certainty. To provide legal certainty for all young people in Austria, that is not possible in the country legislation in this Causa at all. This is neither understandable nor after- For young people, mobility and freedom are included in the framework of youth protection and non-federalist harassment. It's not everyday reality. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Vienna, Lower Austria and Burgenland have managed to unify this. Other federal states are lush. It is nice and also good to hear that the Styria will follow. But that only means that there will be an east-west gap, which is even stronger than now. The East can say on its own: We have uniform rules. The West then says: We continue to consider how “we” – from the country’s point of view – deal with young people. This is a celebration of the competences which are not comprehensible for young people, as has already been said. There is no solid basis for the fact that politicians in the countries – why – believe that they are closer to young people and thus better know what young people need. There is no solid basis for young people in Salzburg to need more restrictive provisions than young people in Burgenland or in Tyrol. There is also no solid basis for national politicians to put themselves on the question of freedom of violence through federal laws. I find it very embarrassing that a country politician can publicly say that sometimes a dance would be necessary. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! A dance is a dance – and there is nothing to interpret: This is clearly violence against children and young people! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Uwe Scheuch is really retreating! [[Assembly with the Greens and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] United Youth Protection means equal obligations, equal rights for all young people, a transparent law for the whole of Austria that protects young people. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen of the SPÖ! An appeal – and we have already discussed this often – is too little. We have been working on harmonisation for 15 years, for 20 years, on a federal law. Apples are not enough. I believe that we should simply take the next step together, as we will find a uniform federal regime on the part of Parliament. To put young people at the centre, this Federal Government should finally perceive.
